# Ariun Nuur — research log

> One file per location (`Research_Logs/README.md`; `00_RUNBOOK.md` Step 3 §3.7, L2628–2643). Append only. Every search string verbatim,
> sources used and rejected, what came back and which finding it became, dead ends (and whether each died at the query or at the
> sources), and open threads. Created 2026-11-18 13:19 by the ULM run of 2026-11-18.

---

## Entry 1 · 2026-11-18 13:19 · Isolation statement

This log belongs to the ULM run of Ariun Nuur that began 2026-11-18 (startup file
`Universal_Location_Methodology/STARTUP_Ariun_Nuur_ULM_2026-11-18.md`). The run researches Ariun Nuur on its own terms: no other city's
pass, culture, spec, personality or vision file is opened, and no other city is used as a baseline or comparison. A relation named in
Ariun Nuur's own lines is taken from those lines and the shared thing's own record. The research itself begins at Step 3.

---

## Entry 2 · 2026-11-19 06:25–07:36 · Step 3, research aimed at what Step 2 named (ULM run, T8: three readers, one merge)

**Pass and point in the procedure:** the ULM run of Ariun Nuur, Step 3, after the Step 2 spine (`02_Spine.md`) named the deficits and the
targets (its §11, S3-1 to S3-20). **Readers:** three Opus readers, each researching independently under a cap of 150 web calls; Round 2
UNANIMOUS on its second run; Round 3 six CONSISTENT. **Web calls:** readers 256 by their own counts; the session tool's count after the
round, 284 (WebSearch 215, WebFetch 69). **The pass's conclusions are in** `City_Development_Passes/Mirny_Subnet/Ariun_Nuur/03_Research.md`
**(this log holds what was looked at, per the convention).** **Rule for this step:** no Antarctic station or place other than the real
Vostok Station and Lake Vostok was researched by name; facts from sources about another named station are listed below as not used.

### The deficits and questions researched against (from `03_Research.md` §1)

Taken from the spine's §11 (S3-1 to S3-20), its deficits D-1 to D-9 with their addresses, and every row the contract, frame and audit carry to Step 3 (§7). Prioritized by difference, not tier: the targets least like the others (the climate upstream, which is a check rather than texture; thin air; the moving ice; knowledge from records; a facility that cannot be left) were taken first among equals, and every target was looked at far enough to know what it would give (3.3). Every target was queried from at least two angles; picks already logged as covered (the gene-editing institute's staff life, one materials platform after June 2026, another's renewal, the light source's night-shift feature, the host laboratory's staff housing) were gone back to (A, B, C).

| # | Target | Angles queried (union) | What it gave | Src |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S3-1 | Food on an ice sheet (D-1; RF-37) | the real station's supply; frozen storage; indoor growing and its energy; the social life of a resupply; a substitute institution (communities with one resupply a year; common cold stores) | FIND-12, FIND-13, FIND-20, FIND-37 | A T1 · B T101 · C T-201 |

### What came back, and which finding it supports (from `03_Research.md` §3.1; sources with links)

**Marks:** SOURCED, read on a fetched page or viewed document; UNVERIFIED, from a search tool's summary only; COMPUTED, a reader's arithmetic on sourced inputs, basis stated. **Outcome** (runbook 3.4): **R** close resemblance with new paint · **M** one mechanism taken, divergence elsewhere · **S** somewhere the source never went · **SITE** a fact about the real site (G2), not a comparable · **DATA** a provenance or data check. Every fact carries its URL as the reader logged it.

## Entry 3 · 2026-11-19 13:32–14:04 · Phase 10R, research supplement on the three official picks (ULM run, T8: three readers, one merge)

**Pass and point in the procedure.** The ULM run on Ariun Nuur, Phase 10 (catalog), process B: the real-world research against the
city's pick list, run as a separate unit after Phases 2–9 were recorded, because the three Phase 10 readers ran short of context before
their research was deep. The picks are the three official ones: Materials Innovation Platforms and ChemMatCARS (secondary), then CRISPR
(primary, read as gene-editing research sites). The city's longer candidate list was not on this unit's reading list. **Readers:** three
Opus readers (A, B, C), each capped at 120 web calls; by their own counts they made 113, 110 and 103. **Round 2:** UNANIMOUS. **Round 3:**
all six cross-verdicts CONSISTENT. **Boundary:** no Antarctic site other than the real Vostok Station and Lake Vostok was researched, and
research on the real station covers the site, never its operator. Real-world places below are named as sources only, never as in-world
places. Every fact is a real-world fact; none is a conclusion about the city. **Merged 2026-11-20 by the merge rule (union where readers
agree or one reader alone found a fact; the most conservative current view where they contradict). PROPOSED; not declared complete.**

### 1 · Facts, per pick then per sub-part

**Phase 10 headings:** **P** named places and landmarks · **T** physical things · **R** people as role-archetypes (no real person's name is
carried; every person is a role) · **S** settings (recurring situations) · **E** significant events (recurring or one-time). "(lost)" marks
what a pick lost (closures, ended programs, retired things); "(unit)" marks a smallest unit. **Marks:** SOURCED = read on the page by a
reader; UNVERIFIED = seen only in a search summary or secondhand. A row that mixes the two says which part is which. **Found by** gives
each finder with the reader's own number (A's 1–88, B's F101–F175, C's F-201–F-260). Pick order follows the readers' brief: the materials
platforms and ChemMatCARS first, then CRISPR, then the brief's further real-world threads.

#### 1.1 Materials Innovation Platforms [SECONDARY]

**Program level (all platforms)**

| # | The fact | Phase 10 heading | Mark | URL(s) | Found by (readers' own numbers) |

#### 1.4 The brief's further real-world threads (not a pick)

**Paper and photographs moved from deep cold into a warm room**

| # | The fact | Phase 10 heading | Mark | URL(s) | Found by (readers' own numbers) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P10R-F142 | Large archives keep an acclimation room; without one, or from freezers, film is left 24 hours before it comes out of its packaging; vault temperatures should not cross the freeze-thaw line | T · S | SOURCED | https://www.archives.gov/preservation/storage/cold-storage | A 75 |
| P10R-F143 | Warming from cold storage (page reviewed 2025-02-14): an acclimation room between vault and reading room, with two doors: "Open the outside acclimation room door and shut it completely before opening the vault door"; under five photographs about one hour, a shoe-box about four hours, a reel about eight hours, larger boxes overnight; sealed packages from freezers or from non-humidity-controlled storage "eight hours to overnight", kept wrapped until they feel room temperature; "wipe off any moisture condensation on the bag prior to opening the bag"; "Never attempt to speed up warming of photographs by placing them near heat"; cold photographs and film are brittle; records brought in from outside acclimate several days (single items) or a week (boxes) before going into cold storage | T · S | SOURCED | https://www.archives.gov/preservation/storage/cold-storage-photos.html | B F166 · C F-251 · A 76 (raised from UNVERIFIED; see Splits settled) |
| P10R-F144 | "a rapid lowering of temperature can result in condensation, causing absorbent materials such as paper to become damp"; paper keeps best at 30–50% relative humidity | T | SOURCED | https://chinapreservationtutorial.library.cornell.edu/content/climate-control | A 77 |
| P10R-F145 | A cold book brought into a warm room gathers enough condensation to warp it; packages are left to reach room temperature before opening | S | UNVERIFIED | https://www.librarything.nl/topic/330558 ; https://cool.culturalheritage.org/byform/mailing-lists/cdl/1999/1409.html | A 78 |

| P10R-F147 | Moisture forms on the edges of cold paper unwrapped in a warm pressroom; 8–36 hours inside the original packaging, overnight common; bagged frozen items thawed sealed for at least 48 hours, with condensation on the outside of the bag only | T | UNVERIFIED | https://zxcprint.com/printing-knowledge/paper-handling.html ; https://www.klug-conservation.com/paper-storage ; https://content.slq.qld.gov.au/sites/default/files/Caring%20for%20your%20collections%20-%20Freezing%20water%20damaged%20and%20insect%20infested%20collections_2025_0.pdf | B F168 |

**Withheld under the orchestrator's point 4** (no fact is written whose source describes an Antarctic station other than the real
Vostok Station): **B F172** (the real site sits "almost in the center of the southern auroral oval", from a 2021 paper whose study route
starts at another station; B logged that source as not used, call 171); **the second sentence of C F-256** (1957–59 camera records of
polar-cap auroras, stated jointly for the real station and another location). Both are recorded here so they are not re-searched blind.

## Entry 4 · 2026-11-20 05:07–05:45 · Phase 10R2 sub-unit 1, candidate research on rows 1–29 (LAW 0-P; ULM run, T8: three readers, one merge)

**Pass and point in the procedure.** The ULM run on Ariun Nuur, Phase 10, process B, run under LAW 0-P after Phase 10R: web research on
candidate rows 1–29 of the city's candidate list (lines 36–64), sub-unit 1 of 3. **Readers:** three Opus readers (A, B, C), working
independently from one frozen brief, each capped at 90 web calls; by their own counts A 88, B 89, C 90, **total 267**. **Round 2:**
UNANIMOUS. **Round 3:** six cross-verdicts, all CONSISTENT. **No Antarctic site other than the real Vostok Station and Lake Vostok was
researched;** one search return about another Antarctic site was logged as not used, without its name. Every real-world place below is a
research source, never a place in the city or the city's equal. This entry records real-world facts and what was looked at; it holds no
conclusion about the city (the Phase 10 merge decides what, if anything, enters the catalog). **Merged 2026-11-20; PROPOSED; not declared
complete.**

**How to read the tables.** SOURCED = read on the page a reader fetched (or on a PDF the fetch tool saved and the reader viewed page by
page; see developer question U1-Q1). UNVERIFIED = seen only in a search summary or secondhand. "Found by" gives each reader's own fact
number with its letter, because the readers' numbers collide (reader A's facts run F1–F193, past its assigned range). A fact whose URL is
not in its finder's own call log is written UNVERIFIED with the note *URL not in the finder's call log*. A fact with no URL is not written.
Tags: `WAITS (CST)` (drinking scene, food customs, music, festivals, games, dress, faith) and `WAITS (DCI)` (courts, fines, law,
governance, administration); a tagged fact is written in full. Recorded conflicts are written in the fact's own row as "split: …".

| U1-F262 | In a former zinc mine at Kamioka, 1,000 m underground; the first detector (1983, built for proton decay) held 3,000 metric tons of water and 1,000 photomultiplier tubes and caught 11 neutrinos from supernova 1987A in about 13 seconds; its successor (April 1996) holds 50,000 metric tons and 11,200 tubes; a next one of about 1 million metric tons is being built | physical thing | SOURCED | https://www.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/utokyo-research/feature-stories/3281-feet-under/index.html | | C F315 |
| U1-F263 | A physics reference page's account of the 12 November 2001 accident: while the tank was being refilled after burned-out tubes were replaced, one tube imploded (microfractures from workers standing on foam pads) and a chain reaction destroyed about 7,000 of 11,000 photomultiplier tubes (a rounded secondary figure; the institute's own count is in U1-F261); about 5,200 survived and about 1,000 spares remained; tubes were to be housed in fiber-reinforced acrylic; full repair was projected at five years and $15–25 million; the detector lay in an active zinc mine | event | SOURCED | https://hyperphysics.gsu.edu/hbase/Particles/kamio.html | | A F190 |
| U1-F264 | The observatory was founded in 1983 for the first detector and renamed in 1995 (one reader's snippet gives 1995 as the founding year; split settled for 1983); after the accident, rebuilding ran from April 2002 over about half a year; a 1.7 km horizontal tunnel is driven to the detector, allowing access around the clock for maintenance | event | UNVERIFIED | https://www-sk.icrr.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/sk/about/history ; https://www.icrr.u-tokyo.ac.jp/prwps/about/history_eng.html ; https://research.kek.jp/group/www-ps/kekps/eppc/AR/PDF2002/2002K2K.pdf ; https://link.springer.com/article/10.1140/epjc/s10052-019-6796-2 | | A F192, C F316 |

## Entry 5 · 2026-11-20 05:07–05:40 · Phase 10R2 sub-unit 2, candidate research on rows 30–58 (LAW 0-P; ULM run, T8: three readers, one merge)

**Pass and point in the procedure.** ULM pass on Ariun Nuur, Phase 10, process B, run under LAW 0-P after Phase 10R: web research on rows 30–58 of the city's candidate list (candidate-list lines 65–93), every row whatever its tier. **Readers:** three Opus readers (A, B, C), each capped at 90 web calls; A 90, B 90, C 86; total 266 by their own counts. **Round 2:** UNANIMOUS. **Round 3:** six cross-verdicts, all CONSISTENT. **Rule held:** no Antarctic site other than the real Vostok Station and Lake Vostok was researched. Real-world places below are research sources, never places in the city; nothing here is a conclusion about the city, and the Phase 10 merge decides what, if anything, enters the catalog. **Merged 2026-11-20; PROPOSED; not declared complete.**

**How to read the tables.** SOURCED = read on a page a reader fetched; UNVERIFIED = seen only in a search tool's summary or secondhand. A fact read on the page by one reader and seen only in a summary by another is SOURCED, and its first URL is the page read. "Found by" gives each reader's own fact number with its reader letter (A's run F1–F150, B's F101–F246, C's F201–F347; B's and C's numbers overlap, so the letter is part of the number). A fact whose URL is not in its finder's own call log is UNVERIFIED and says so. A tagged fact (WAITS (CST) or WAITS (DCI)) is written in full. Where a source used a word in its own language, the fact is kept in English and the word is dropped.

| U3-F59 | The physicist "founded a large cryogenics laboratory" in Leiden in 1904; helium was first liquefied there on 10 July 1908 (boiling point about 4.2 K, −269 °C), then taken to "a temperature near 1.5 K" by lowering the pressure, "the lowest temperature recorded on Earth at the time"; on 8 April 1911 mercury's resistance vanished at 3.6 K "in a solid mercury wire immersed in liquid helium" ("superconductive state"); Nobel Prize 1913; his student and successor as director, Willem Hendrik Keesom, first solidified helium in 1926; the old laboratory building now houses the university's Law Faculty (the Kamerlingh Onnes Gebouw) | named place · event | SOURCED | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heike_Kamerlingh_Onnes | | A F28, B F123, B F124; C F240 (building, UNVERIFIED) |

*pointer:* F8. **Doubt "whether the marker exists": the monument near Ürümqi exists, but it marks a computed geographic center of Asia (1992), not the pole of inaccessibility; no source found any marker at the pole itself** (U3-F202, U3-F205).

Reader B cites cavac.at mirror pages in two UNVERIFIED facts (U3-F26, U3-F65); one clause rests on a mirror alone (U3-F26: Poker Flat called the only university-owned rocket range), and U3-F65's mirror URL is not in its finder's call log.

### Developer questions (PROVISIONAL, each with a recommended answer)

| # | Question | Raised by | Recommended answer (PROVISIONAL) |
|---|---|---|---|
| U3-Q1 | **Period and people adjectives under the no-demonym rule.** Does the rule cover words such as "Roman" (as in "a Roman villa"), "Viking" (the row's own name, used by all three readers) and "American spelling"? | Reader C, Round 3 (marked (c)) | A real place's proper name keeps its words as a source name (for example, the Viking Ship Museum). Elsewhere, write the period plainly, by its date, where the source dates it ("a 4th-century villa wing"; a longship built about 1042); where the source gives no date, keep the source's phrase in the fact and flag it. "American spelling" names a spelling convention, not a people, and stays. This merge applies that answer throughout. |
| U3-Q2 | **Facts read from a PDF that the fetch tool saved and the reader then viewed with Read.** Reader A (call 71, slides 1–8) and reader C (call 68, pages 1–4; call 70, slides 1–12) marked such facts SOURCED (U3-F32, F34, F35, F175). Is a saved copy of a logged fetch a "read on the page fetched," and does its URL count as in the finder's own call record? | The orchestrator's Round 3 points (the saved-PDF point) | Yes: keep them SOURCED. The URL is in each finder's own fetch log, the bytes came from that logged fetch, and the readers viewed the saved file as web data, not as a repo file. Mark each such fact "SOURCED (PDF viewed)," as this entry does, so the route stays visible. |
